| `app.api.idempotency.in-flight-seconds` | `APP_API_IDEMPOTENCY_IN_FLIGHT_SECONDS` | `300`. How long a key stays claimed by a request that is still running (a repeat meanwhile gets 409). Raise it if a covered request can legitimately run longer |
| `app.api.deprecation-policy-url` | `APP_API_DEPRECATION_POLICY_URL` | The deprecation policy on GitHub. Target of the `Link: <...>; rel="deprecation"` header on deprecated routes; point it at your own docs when you host them |

## Mail and channels

SMTP (`SMTP_HOST`, `SMTP_PORT`, `SMTP_USERNAME`, `SMTP_PASSWORD`, read by the
`workflow` and `lite` profiles) is used for notifications and email steps. Channel credentials (WhatsApp, Telegram, Slack,
Teams, ...) are configured per tenant in the console and stored encrypted, not
in environment variables.

| Property | Env | Default | |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.omnichannel.sms.max-reply-chars` | `SMS_MAX_REPLY_CHARS` | `1500` | longest SMS reply sent; longer agent replies are cut at a sentence or word boundary with an ellipsis (Twilio's ceiling is 1,600, clamped). See [SMS conversations](sms-channel.md). |
| `app.omnichannel.rate-limit.enabled` | (none) | `false` (`true` in the `personal` profile). Master switch for the inbound turn cap (see [Personal agent settings](#personal-agent-settings)) |
| `app.signal.*` | (none) | Signal receive loop and limits: [Signal channel](channels/signal.md) |
| `app.matrix.sync.enabled` | `APP_MATRIX_SYNC_ENABLED` | `false`. Matrix receives nothing until this is `true`; see [Matrix channel](matrix-channel.md) |
| `app.imessage.*` | (none) | iMessage polling and rate limits: [iMessage channel](imessage-channel.md) |
| `app.omnichannel.edge-relay.*` | `EDGE_RELAY_*` | enabled | a signed-in browser with an on-device model answers the workspace's SMS and WhatsApp messages; every knob is in [Edge relay](edge-relay.md) |

## Security hardening settings

Core properties added by the pre-release security review
([docs/engineering/SECURITY-HARDENING.md](engineering/SECURITY-HARDENING.md)
has the rationale and the behaviour each one changes). They are Spring
properties: set them as environment variables with the relaxed-binding names
below. Compose passes only the first one through (`x-core-env`); add the others
to `core.env` or the Helm `core.env` map when you need them.

| Property | Environment variable | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `app.security.outbound.allowed-private-hosts` | `APP_SECURITY_OUTBOUND_ALLOWED_PRIVATE_HOSTS` | empty; comma-separated `host` or `host:port` that tenant-configured outbound calls (BYOK, OpenAPI, A2A, webhooks) may reach on a private address, e.g. `ollama:11434`. Also needed for a `LOCAL_OPENAI` server, a Signal `signal-cli` service, a BlueBubbles Mac, a LAN Matrix homeserver and an email (IMAP) mailbox on a private address |
| `app.security.outbound.egress-allow-list` | `APP_SECURITY_OUTBOUND_EGRESS_ALLOW_LIST` | empty (any public host); comma-separated `host` or `*.suffix`. When set, tenant-configured outbound calls (BYOK, OpenAPI, MCP URLs, web fetch, webhooks) may reach only these hosts; the private-address checks still apply and `allowed-private-hosts` entries are exempt. Platform-wide, not per agent |
| `app.tenancy.fail-on-missing-context` | `APP_TENANCY_FAIL_ON_MISSING_CONTEXT` | `true`; `false` restores the shared-schema fallback (logged) |
| `app.security.jwt.required-audience` | `APP_SECURITY_JWT_REQUIRED_AUDIENCE` | empty (off); needs a Keycloak audience mapper first |
| `app.oauth.state.require-browser-cookie` | `APP_OAUTH_STATE_REQUIRE_BROWSER_COOKIE` | `false` |
| `app.agent.share.visitor-token-secret` | `APP_AGENT_SHARE_VISITOR_TOKEN_SECRET` | empty: derived from `APP_ENCRYPTION_KEY` |
| `app.storage.local.legacy-unprefixed-fallback` | `APP_STORAGE_LOCAL_LEGACY_UNPREFIXED_FALLBACK` | `false` |
| `app.sso.tenant-idp.first-broker-login-flow` | `APP_SSO_TENANT_IDP_FIRST_BROKER_LOGIN_FLOW` | `first broker login` |
| `app.sharing.default-ttl` | `APP_SHARING_DEFAULT_TTL` | `P30D` for new share links |
| `app.chat.session-ttl-hours` | `APP_CHAT_SESSION_TTL_HOURS` | `24`; how long a conversation (and its owner record) stays in Redis |

The console's `NEXT_PUBLIC_OAUTH_STATE_COOKIE` (web) pairs with
`app.oauth.state.require-browser-cookie`: set both when turning the binding
cookie on.

### MCP specification alignment settings

Added with [MCP specification alignment](architecture/mcp-spec-alignment.md), which has the rationale and the
requirement-by-requirement status. Spring properties; the first three are in `application-mcpserver.yml`
(active with the `mcpserver` profile), the last two in `application.yml`.

| Property | Environment variable | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `app.mcpserver.origin.mode` | `APP_MCPSERVER_ORIGIN_MODE` | `enforce`; `report` logs a disallowed `Origin` and lets it through, `off` disables the check. Requests with no `Origin` header (every non-browser MCP client) are never affected |
| `app.mcpserver.origin.allowed` | `APP_MCPSERVER_ORIGIN_ALLOWED` | empty; comma-separated exact origins (`https://app.example.com`), no wildcards. The request's own `Host` is not trusted |
| `app.mcpserver.oauth.*` | `APP_MCPSERVER_OAUTH_ENABLED`, `_RESOURCE`, `_ISSUER`, `_AUTHORIZATION_SERVERS`, `_JWK_SET_URI`, `_TENANT_CLAIM`, `_GRANT_CLAIM` | disabled. OAuth 2.1 access tokens for `/mcp`, next to the `vmcp_` grant keys (which keep working). Needs `resource` (canonical https URI of `/mcp`), `issuer` and `jwk-set-uri` (each must be https, or http on a loopback host for development; a missing or plain-http non-loopback value fails startup). `_AUTHORIZATION_SERVERS` (comma-separated) lists the authorization servers advertised in the RFC 9728 Protected Resource Metadata (`/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource`); blank advertises the `issuer` The token must carry the tenant claim (`tenant_id`) and a grant claim (`mcp_grant_id`, UUID of an enabled grant); its `aud` must contain `resource`. Scopes are exact-match, no wildcards; see the alignment doc for the scope table |
| `app.mcp.tool-pinning.mode` | `APP_MCP_TOOL_PINNING_MODE` | `warn`. `off` records nothing; `warn` records a hash of each remote MCP server's tool list, audits a change (`mcp.tools.changed`) and lists it at `GET /api/mcp/tool-pins`, agents keep using the server; `enforce` also removes a changed server's tools from agents until an admin calls `POST /api/mcp/tool-pins/{id}/approve` |
| `app.mcp.client.routing-headers.enabled` | `APP_MCP_CLIENT_ROUTING_HEADERS_ENABLED` | `false`; sends `Mcp-Method` / `Mcp-Name` on outbound Streamable HTTP POSTs |

### Local LLM pool bootstrap

Registers a local Ollama (or any OpenAI-compatible local server) as the FREE-tier platform pool at startup, with no manual API calls. Off by default; runs once in the api shape only (the jobs and worker shapes skip it), never fails startup (problems are logged at WARN), and is idempotent: a pool row with the same label or base URL is left untouched. The row is created through the normal pool service (key encrypted with `APP_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, SSRF guard applied), enabled, ahead of every existing row. A private host such as `ollama:11434` must also be listed in `app.security.outbound.allowed-private-hosts`. If the model is not pulled, the row is still created and a WARN gives the exact `ollama pull <model>` command.

| Property | Environment variable | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `app.local-llm.bootstrap.enabled` | `APP_LOCAL_LLM_BOOTSTRAP_ENABLED` | `false` |
| `app.local-llm.bootstrap.base-url` | `APP_LOCAL_LLM_BOOTSTRAP_BASE_URL` | `http://ollama:11434` |
| `app.local-llm.bootstrap.model` | `APP_LOCAL_LLM_BOOTSTRAP_MODEL` | `qwen3.5:4b` (the same default the CLI pulls) |
| `app.local-llm.bootstrap.label` | `APP_LOCAL_LLM_BOOTSTRAP_LABEL` | `Local model (Ollama)` |
| `app.local-llm.bootstrap.api-key` | `APP_LOCAL_LLM_BOOTSTRAP_API_KEY` | `ollama` (a placeholder Ollama ignores); set it empty for a keyless row that sends no `Authorization` header |
| `app.local-llm.bootstrap.retry-delay-ms` | (none) | `10000`; delay between up to 6 attempts while the server is unreachable (Ollama often starts after core) |

## Personal agent settings

Settings added with [personal mode](personal-mode.md). Every default below leaves an existing install
unchanged (except the six agent-runtime defaults set on 2026-10-23, see [Agent and platform feature flags](#agent-and-platform-feature-flags)); the `personal` profile (`application-personal.yml`) flips several of them, listed first. All are
Spring properties, so the environment variable is the relaxed-binding name (`app.auth.mode` is
`APP_AUTH_MODE`). Compose passes only the ones named in `deploy/compose/personal.yml`; put the rest in
`deploy/compose/core.env`.

### What the `personal` profile changes

| Property | Env | Without `personal` | With `personal` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.auth.mode` | `APP_AUTH_MODE` | `keycloak` | `local` |
| `app.agents.taint.mode` | `APP_AGENTS_TAINT_MODE` | `observe` (`off` before 2026-10-23) | `enforce` |
| `app.agent-hooks.enabled` | `AGENT_HOOKS_ENABLED` | `true` (`false` before 2026-10-23) | `false` (nobody there authors hooks) |
| `app.tool-arg-policies.enabled` | `TOOL_ARG_POLICIES_ENABLED` | `true` (`false` before 2026-10-23) | `false` (nobody there authors policies) |
| `app.skills.supply-chain.enforce` | `SKILLS_SUPPLY_CHAIN_ENFORCE` | `true` (`false` before 2026-10-23) | `false` (scan and pins stay on) |
| `app.mcp.stdio.sandbox` | `APP_MCP_STDIO_SANDBOX` | `none` | `os` (the container stack sets `none`, see [Personal mode](personal-mode.md#sandbox-levels)) |
| `app.skills.learning.enabled` | `APP_SKILLS_LEARNING_ENABLED` | `false` | `true` |
| `app.skills.learning.self-check.enabled` | `APP_SKILLS_LEARNING_SELF_CHECK_ENABLED` | `false` | `true` |
| `app.learning.proposals.enabled` | `APP_LEARNING_PROPOSALS_ENABLED` | `false` | `true` |
| `app.learning.lessons.enabled` | `APP_LEARNING_LESSONS_ENABLED` | `false` | `true` |
| `app.learning.preferences.enabled` | `APP_LEARNING_PREFERENCES_ENABLED` | `false` | `true` |
| `app.learning.routines.enabled` | `APP_LEARNING_ROUTINES_ENABLED` | `false` | `true` |
| `app.learning.review.enabled` | `APP_LEARNING_REVIEW_ENABLED` | `false` | `true` |
| `app.edition.tier` | `APP_EDITION_TIER` | unset (every [edition feature](#edition-tier-and-features) on) | `personal` (the Compose stack derives it from `VEGADUTA_EDITION`) |
| `app.edition.billing-enabled` | `APP_EDITION_BILLING_ENABLED` | `true` | `false` |
| `app.a2a.enabled` | `APP_A2A_ENABLED` | `true` | `false` |
| `resolution.sweep.enabled` | `RESOLUTION_SWEEP_ENABLED` | `true` | `false` |
| `app.scheduled-tasks.delivery-channels` | `SCHEDULED_TASKS_DELIVERY_CHANNELS` | the shared list | the shared list without `slack` and `a2a` |
| `app.omnichannel.rate-limit.enabled` | `APP_OMNICHANNEL_RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED` | `false` | `true` |
| `app.embeddings.provider` | `APP_EMBEDDINGS_PROVIDER` | `sidecar` | `auto` (cloud key, else Ollama, else the sidecar) |
| `app.sidecar.enabled` | `APP_SIDECAR_ENABLED` | `true` | `false`; the launcher's `--profile rag` sets it to `true`. With Compose, put an override in `deploy/compose/.env`, not `core.env` |

The profile also sets tracing off, `app.storage.type=local`, `app.workflow.execution-engine=legacy`, a 2 s Redis
timeout, Hikari pool `DB_HIKARI_MAX_POOL_SIZE` (10) and `DB_HIKARI_MIN_IDLE` (2), and the mail and Telegram
blocks that normally ship in the `workflow` profile (Telegram long polling on). Redis (Valkey) stays: about 60
classes use it directly, so `personal.yml` runs a 128 MB one (a rate-limit counter evicted under pressure fails open, so it keeps headroom).

**The lite tier** (`up --lite`) sits between the two: Keycloak auth like the full stack, one box like
personal. `lite.yml` ships the Assistant's features on for it - `SCHEDULED_TASKS_ENABLED`,
`APP_CHAT_BRANCHING_ENABLED`, `APP_CHAT_RESUMABLE_STREAMS_ENABLED`, `DATA_TABLES_ENABLED`,
`APP_SKILLS_LEARNING_*`/`APP_LEARNING_*` and the `BROWSER_V2/PROFILES/TAKEOVER` flags (each can be set back to
`false` in `.env`) - and accepts the single-box compose profiles `rag`, `tools`, `workspace` and the `--search`
/`--browser` flags. `APP_SIDECAR_ENABLED` defaults `false` there (the `--profile rag` run sets it true) and
`APP_EMBEDDINGS_PROVIDER=auto` falls back to a cloud key or a registered Ollama when no sidecar runs.

Compose and launcher variables used by personal mode (none required in `.env`; the CLI generates the secrets):

| Variable | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `PERSONAL_SPRING_PROFILES` | `security,knowledge,graph-memory,personal` | profile set for core |
| `PERSONAL_JAVA_OPTS_EXTRA` | empty | appended to the core JVM flags (later flags win), e.g. `-Xmx700m` |
| `PERSONAL_AOT_CACHE` | `false` | with `--build`, passes `ENABLE_AOT_CACHE=true` to `core/Dockerfile` (an AppCDS archive: faster boot, larger image) |
| `POSTGRES_MEM_LIMIT` / `CORE_MEM_LIMIT` / `WEB_MEM_LIMIT` | `384m` / `1536m` / `384m` | container memory limits in `personal.yml` |
| `VEGADUTA_EDITION` | `personal` | `personal` \| `lite` \| `full`. The one switch for the edition: becomes core's `APP_EDITION_TIER` and web's `NEXT_PUBLIC_EDITION`, so the two cannot disagree. The launcher writes `personal` into `.env` and never replaces a value you set. See [Edition tier and features](#edition-tier-and-features) |
| `WEB_AUTH_MODE` | `local` | becomes `NEXT_PUBLIC_AUTH_MODE` on web |
| `WEB_BILLING_ENABLED` | empty (off) | becomes `NEXT_PUBLIC_BILLING_ENABLED`; billing UI is hidden unless `1` |
| `VEGADUTA_PERSONAL_HOME` | `~/.vegaduta/personal` | state for `--native-core`: core log, PID file, file storage (mode 700) |
| `VEGADUTA_CORE_JAR` | unset | jar for `--native-core`; else `--build` builds it, else it is copied out of the release's core image |
| `VEGADUTA_CORE_HEAP` | `1g` | `-Xmx` of the host JVM (SerialGC) |
| `LOCAL_LLM_BASE_URL_NATIVE` | `http://127.0.0.1:11434` | Ollama address the host JVM uses with `--local-ai` |
| `POSTGRES_HOST_PORT` / `VALKEY_HOST_PORT` | `5432` / `6379` | loopback-only ports published by `personal.native.yml` for the host JVM |
| `APP_MCP_STDIO_SANDBOX` | `none` in `personal.yml` | overrides the sandbox level of the container core |

### Local sign-in (`app.auth.mode=local`)

Core is its own identity provider for one owner: RS256 tokens, rotating refresh tokens, no Keycloak. Keycloak
mode is unchanged when this is left at `keycloak`. Needs the `security` profile and `APP_WEB_PUBLIC_URL`.

| Property | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `app.auth.mode` | `keycloak` | `local` enables it. Blank means `keycloak` |
| `app.auth.local.data-dir` | `${user.home}/.vegaduta/auth` | signing keys (`signing-keys.json`, file `0600`, directory `0700`) and the one-time `first-run-setup.txt`. Keep it on a persistent volume: losing the key signs everyone out. One core process per directory. `personal.yml` sets `APP_AUTH_LOCAL_DATA_DIR=/data/storage/.auth` |
| `app.auth.local.issuer` / `.audience` | `https://auth.local.vegaduta.invalid` / `vegaduta-api` | `iss` and `aud` of the tokens; the issuer is an identifier nobody dereferences |
| `app.auth.local.access-token-ttl` | `15m` | access token lifetime |
| `app.auth.local.refresh-token-ttl` | `30d` | idle lifetime of one refresh token |
| `app.auth.local.session-max-ttl` | `90d` | absolute lifetime of a login; refreshing never extends it |
| `app.auth.local.key-rotation-interval` | `90d` | the key is rotated lazily when older than this |
| `app.auth.local.key-retention` | `1d` | how long a retired key still verifies (floored at twice the access TTL) |
| `app.auth.local.setup-token-ttl` | `24h` | validity of the first-run and invite links |
| `app.auth.local.owner-email` | `owner@vegaduta.local` | sign-in e-mail of the first-run owner |
| `app.auth.local.owner-first-name` / `app.auth.local.owner-last-name` | `Owner` / `Account` | name of that owner |
| `app.auth.local.tenant-name` | `Personal` | name of the default tenant |
| `app.auth.local.owner-extra-roles` | empty | extra roles for the owner, e.g. `platform-admin` to reach `/api/platform/**` |
| `app.auth.local.passkey.origins` / `APP_AUTH_LOCAL_PASSKEY_ORIGINS` | empty (any acceptable origin) | optional comma-separated allow-list of console origins that may register or use passkeys, for example `https://console.example.com,http://localhost:3000`. When set, passkey options for any other origin are refused with `400 passkey_origin_unsupported`. Without it core accepts `https://<host>[:port]` or `http://localhost[:port]` and refuses IP-address hosts (WebAuthn forbids an IP as relying-party ID). The relying-party ID is the origin's host and is stored with each passkey. The console's proxy forwards the browser's own origin in `X-Vegaduta-Origin` (core falls back to `Origin`). Read only when `app.auth.mode=local` |

Web (console) variables: `NEXT_PUBLIC_AUTH_MODE` (`keycloak` default, `local` to switch the sign-in page) and, server
side only, `CORE_INTERNAL_URL` (where the console's same-origin auth proxy reaches core; falls back to
`NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL`). Setting `app.auth.mode=local` also sets `app.identity.provider=local` (override with
`IDENTITY_PROVIDER`). Passwords: 12 characters minimum, 72 bytes maximum. Passkeys, PINs (6 to 12 digits, bound to
a per-browser device secret; 10 wrong PINs revoke the device), recovery codes and the 10-minute freshness rule for
changing them are fixed limits, not settings; the tables are `control_plane.local_passkey`, `local_device` and
`local_recovery_code` (V655). The console's proxy keeps the device secret in the HttpOnly cookie `vd_device`
(`vd_device_label` holds its name; both `SameSite=Strict`, `Path=/api/local-auth`, 400 days, `Secure` when the
browser reached the console over HTTPS directly or through `X-Forwarded-Proto`). Details and limits are in
[Personal mode](personal-mode.md#local-sign-in).

### Day-one defaults and starter connectors (personal)

Read by `scripts/vegaduta personal` and the personal profile; see
[What is on from the start](personal-mode.md#what-is-on-from-the-start).

| Variable / property | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `VEGADUTA_SEARCH` | unset (on for a fresh personal install) | `0`, `off`, `false` or `no` in `.env` or the environment turns private web search off; `1`, `on`, `true` or `yes` forces it on. A `--search` / `--no-search` flag wins for that run; this wins over the remembered answer (`search=` in `.vegaduta-state`). Read by the launcher only (`scripts\vegaduta.ps1` too, where web search is off unless `-Search` or this asks for it) |
| `VEGADUTA_TOOLS` | unset (on for a fresh personal install) | the same for the MCP runner (compose profile `tools`). Not used with `--native-core` |
| `VEGADUTA_VALKEY` | unset (always on) | `0` leaves the Valkey container out of a **personal** install; rate limits, budget windows and spend counters run in core's bounded in-process fallback (they stay enforced and fail closed). `--no-valkey` / `--valkey` wins for that run; this wins over the remembered answer (`valkey=` in `.vegaduta-state`). Ignored on lite/full - shared installs keep the shared store |
| `app.mcp.starter.enabled` / `APP_MCP_STARTER_ENABLED` | `true` in the `personal` profile (the class is not loaded elsewhere) | `false` registers no starter connectors even when the runner is on. They are also registered only when `MCP_STDIO_SIDECAR_ENABLED=true` |
| `MCP_STDIO_SIDECAR_ENABLED` | `false`; the launcher exports `true` with the runner | in `personal.yml` this was hard-coded `false`; it now reads the variable, so a raw `docker compose --profile tools` needs it in `.env` |
| `MCP_STDIO_RUNNER_MEM_LIMIT` | `768m` in `personal.yml` (`1g` in the full stack) | memory limit of the runner container |
| `MCP_STDIO_RUNNER_MAX_CHILDREN` | `8` in `personal.yml` (`100` in the runner itself) | STDIO servers the runner runs at once; further spawns queue |

`scripts/vegaduta personal [--no-search] [--no-tools]`; `--profile tools` is accepted in personal mode and turns the
runner on (kept as `tools=1|0` in `.vegaduta-state`, never in `profiles=`). Migration V659 (tenant) adds
`mcp_starter_seed(starter_key, seeded_at)`, the record that makes a deleted or edited starter final.

### Add-ons (`/api/addons`)

The add-on registry lists the optional capabilities of a single-box install - personal and lite
(`core/providers/src/main/resources/addons/catalog.json`) and records which ones a person wants; the state is
install-wide in `control_plane.addon_state` (V656). On a lite install the catalog's commands render as
`scripts/vegaduta up --lite ...` (the same profiles and flags exist there); on the full stack the console
does not advertise the page because its service set is fixed at deploy time. Core runs in a container and cannot start containers, so for
add-ons that are compose profiles it only records the wish; the host applies it. Model packs reuse
`app.local-models.*` and the configured Ollama (`app.local-models.ollama-base-url` / `app.local-llm.bootstrap.base-url`).
See [Add-ons](personal-mode.md#add-ons) and the [console guide](console/add-ons.md).

| Property | Environment variable | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.addons.host-token` | `APP_ADDONS_HOST_TOKEN` (Compose: `ADDONS_HOST_TOKEN` in `.env`, passed by `lite.yml` and `docker-compose.yml`; `personal.yml` does not yet) | empty | shared secret the host-side installer sends as `Authorization: Bearer <token>` to `GET /api/addons/host/wanted` and `POST /api/addons/host/report`. At least 32 characters (`openssl rand -hex 32`). Empty or shorter switches the installer surface off (`503 host_bridge_disabled`); there is no unauthenticated fallback and loopback is not trusted. Keep it in `.env`, never in the repo |
| `app.addons.tenant-admin-may-change` | `APP_ADDONS_TENANT_ADMIN_MAY_CHANGE` | `true` when `app.auth.mode=local`, otherwise `false` | whether a tenant admin may switch add-ons on and off. Add-ons are install-wide (a container or a model on the machine), so on a shared install only a platform admin may unless you set this on a self-hosted box you own. Reading the list needs only a signed-in user |

### Browser add-on and routine learning (personal)

| Property | Environment variable | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.browseruse.remote-url` | `APP_BROWSERUSE_REMOTE_URL` | empty | WebSocket address of a Playwright server (the `browser-runner` container of `deploy/compose/browser.yml`, `ws://browser-runner:3000/`). With it set the Assistant's browser is available in `auto` mode without Chromium in the core image; the domain policy, confirmation gates, saved logins and takeover behave as with an in-core Chromium |
| `app.learning.routines.min-distinct-days` | | `3` | a habit must be seen on at least this many different days before it is proposed as a routine |
| `app.learning.routines.retention-days` | | `45` | how long routine observations are kept before the hourly prune deletes them |

### Taint tracking (`app.agents.taint.*`)

| Property | Env | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.agents.taint.mode` | `APP_AGENTS_TAINT_MODE` | `observe` (`enforce` in `personal`; `off` before 2026-10-23, owner decision) | `off`, `observe` (the base default: track, count and audit what would need approval, one audit row and one WARN per tool per tainted turn; behaviour unchanged, but it classifies every guarded tool call and writes `audit_log_entry` rows) or `enforce` (untrusted tool output is delimited as data and, once a turn is tainted, a tool that writes tenant data, uses stored credentials or sends data out needs approval) |
| `app.agents.taint.http-get-allow-hosts` | `APP_AGENTS_TAINT_HTTP_GET_ALLOW_HOSTS` | empty | comma-separated hosts (and their subdomains) a tainted turn may `GET` through `http_request` / `fetch_web_page`; empty means no restriction on GET. Only consulted while a turn is tainted |

Metrics: `agent.taint.marked{tool}`, `agent.taint.tainted_turns{mode}`, `agent.taint.sink_calls{tool,action}`. Audit entries
(actor `agent-taint`): `tool.taint.would_block`, `tool.taint.approval_required`; they carry tool name, risk label and
sources, never arguments or output. See [Security model](security-model.md#taint-tracking).

### STDIO MCP sandbox

The `app.mcp.stdio` properties wrap the dynamic STDIO MCP servers core spawns for tenants. The command guard and env isolation still run first.
`mcp-stdio-sidecar.enabled=true` takes precedence and the sandbox is then not applied a second time.

| Property | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `app.mcp.stdio.sandbox` | `none` | `none`, `os` (macOS `sandbox-exec`; Linux bubblewrap, else a degraded `unshare` fallback; Windows unsupported) or `container` (`docker` / `podman`). Env `APP_MCP_STDIO_SANDBOX` |
| `app.mcp.stdio.sandbox-required` | `true` | mechanism unavailable: refuse spawns (and fail start-up, below). `false` runs the server unsandboxed with a WARN on each spawn |
| `app.mcp.stdio.preflight-fail-fast` | `true` | with a required but unavailable sandbox, `false` lets core start and refuse every spawn instead |
| `app.mcp.stdio.sandbox-allow-degraded` | `true` | allow the Linux `unshare` fallback (no filesystem confinement) |
| `app.mcp.stdio.sandbox-network-allow` | empty | server names (case-insensitive) or `*` that may use the network; empty means none, so `npx -y <package>` cannot download on first run |
| `app.mcp.stdio.sandbox-allow-loopback` | `false` | with network allowed, also let the child connect to 127.0.0.1 and ::1. macOS only; off because Postgres and Valkey are published there in native mode |
| `app.mcp.stdio.sandbox-extra-deny-read` | empty | extra absolute paths hidden from the child: a subpath deny on macOS, a tmpfs (file: `/dev/null`) over the path on Linux bubblewrap. The `personal` profile reads `VEGADUTA_MCP_DENY_READ` into it (the native launcher lists the deploy/compose directory, which holds `.env`). Everything not listed stays readable, see [Security model](security-model.md#sandbox-for-tools-that-run-code) |
| `app.mcp.stdio.sandbox-extra-read-only` | empty | extra paths bound read-only in bubblewrap (e.g. an nvm prefix) |
| `app.mcp.stdio.sandbox-container-runtime` | `auto` | `auto` (docker, then podman), `docker` or `podman` |
| `app.mcp.stdio.sandbox-container-image-node` | `node:22-slim` | image for `node` / `npx` |
| `app.mcp.stdio.sandbox-container-image-python` | `ghcr.io/astral-sh/uv:python3.12-bookworm-slim` | image for `uv` / `uvx` / `python` |
| `app.mcp.stdio.sandbox-container-memory` | `512m` | `--memory` |
| `app.mcp.stdio.sandbox-container-pids` | `256` | `--pids-limit` (floor 16) |
| `app.mcp.stdio.sandbox-container-user` | auto | `uid:gid`; blank uses this process's on POSIX |

`GET /api/system/stdio-sandbox` (any authenticated user) reports `{mode, mechanism, available, degraded, enforcing,
required, networkAllowAll, networkAllowCount, detail}`; no paths, secrets or server names. Static STDIO connections in
`application-mcp.yml` are operator-configured and are not sandboxed by this setting.

The coding harness local runner is separate: `runner=local` needs `app.coding-harness.allow-local-runner=true`,
`app.coding-harness.acknowledge-local-runner-risk=true` (env `APP_CODING_HARNESS_ACKNOWLEDGE_LOCAL_RUNNER_RISK`) and
`server.address` set to a loopback address (unset means all interfaces and is refused). A warning is logged when it is
armed and each time a local sandbox opens.

### The Assistant's terminal

`local_shell_read` and `local_shell` (see [Personal mode](personal-mode.md#the-assistants-terminal)). Offered only
when `enabled` is true, this is a one-person install and the OS sandbox above is on, available and not degraded.

| Property | Env | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.assistant.local-shell.enabled` | `APP_ASSISTANT_LOCAL_SHELL_ENABLED` | `false` (`true` in the `personal` profile) | Offer the two tools |
| `app.assistant.local-shell.approval-required` | `APP_ASSISTANT_LOCAL_SHELL_APPROVAL_REQUIRED` | `true` (`false` in the `personal` profile) | Park every `local_shell` call for the person's approval. `false` lifts the always-confirm pin and the classification confirmation gate for `local_shell` only - commands run inside the sandbox, still logged; a `SUPERVISED`-tier agent is still gated on every call |
| `app.assistant.local-shell.acknowledge-shared-host` | `APP_ASSISTANT_LOCAL_SHELL_ACKNOWLEDGE_SHARED_HOST` | `false` | Allow it outside the `personal` profile; any tenant's agent then runs commands on this host |
| `app.assistant.api-guide.enabled` | `APP_ASSISTANT_API_GUIDE_ENABLED` | `true` | Offer the read-only `vegaduta_api_guide` tool (searches this install's OpenAPI spec) on every install. The spec is served by the API shape, so the jobs and worker shapes never offer the tool |
| `app.assistant.platform-guide.enabled` | `APP_ASSISTANT_PLATFORM_GUIDE_ENABLED` | `true` | Offer the read-only `vegaduta_setup_guide` tool: matches a request to the console page that sets it up, reports live status (MCP servers, channels, Google) and searches the bundled docs |
| `app.assistant.local-shell.workspace` | `APP_ASSISTANT_LOCAL_SHELL_WORKSPACE` | `~/vegaduta-workspace` | Start folder and the only writable path |
| `app.assistant.local-shell.default-timeout-seconds` | `APP_ASSISTANT_LOCAL_SHELL_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | `60` | Time limit when the call names none |
| `app.assistant.local-shell.max-timeout-seconds` | `APP_ASSISTANT_LOCAL_SHELL_MAX_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | `300` | Upper cap on a call's time limit |
| `app.assistant.local-shell.max-output-chars` | `APP_ASSISTANT_LOCAL_SHELL_MAX_OUTPUT_CHARS` | `20000` | Output returned to the model |
| `app.assistant.local-shell.runner-url` | `APP_ASSISTANT_LOCAL_SHELL_RUNNER_URL` | empty | The workspace-runner (`services/workspace-runner`). When set, commands run there instead of the OS sandbox; the launcher's `--profile workspace` sets it to `http://workspace-runner:8090` |
| `app.assistant.local-shell.runner-secret` | `APP_ASSISTANT_LOCAL_SHELL_RUNNER_SECRET` | empty | Bearer secret the runner checks (Compose passes `WORKSPACE_RUNNER_SECRET`, generated by `scripts/vegaduta secrets`) |
| `app.assistant.local-shell.workspace-host-path` | `APP_ASSISTANT_LOCAL_SHELL_WORKSPACE_HOST_PATH` | empty | Where the projects folder is on the host, shown in the Assistant's folder picker |
| `app.guardrails.max-tool-calls-per-turn` | `GUARDRAILS_MAX_TOOL_CALLS_PER_TURN` | `15` (`150` in the `personal` profile) | Tool calls one turn may make (see [Tool calls: budget, rounds and result size](#tool-calls-budget-rounds-and-result-size)) |
| `app.chat.continuation.max-passes` | `APP_CHAT_CONTINUATION_MAX_PASSES` | `8` | Extra model passes a turn (any reasoning depth) may run when a reply stops at an announced-but-undone step ("Let me create the file:"); a pass that makes no tool call ends it. `0` turns it off. When a reply counts as unfinished is described under [conversation-aware turns](#conversation-aware-turns-follow-ups-continuation-and-routing) |
| `app.chat.finish.enabled` | `APP_CHAT_FINISH_ENABLED` | `true` | Group `app.chat.finish`. Act on the provider's finish reason (Anthropic `stop_reason`, OpenAI `finish_reason`, Gemini `finishReason`, Ollama `done_reason`): continue a reply cut off at the output limit, say so when the provider's safety system stopped a reply, retry an unusable tool call once. `false` ignores finish reasons, as before. An empty reply or one closed after a failure is never cached or stored as an answer whatever this says |
| `app.chat.finish.max-continuations` | `APP_CHAT_FINISH_MAX_CONTINUATIONS` | `2` | Extra model passes allowed to finish a reply cut off at the output limit (`length` / `max_tokens`). Each is billed like any call and offers no tools. The pieces are joined; if the reply is still cut off the person gets `truncated-notice` and the reply is kept in the transcript as cut off (not cached). `0` never continues |
| `app.chat.finish.max-tool-call-retries` | `APP_CHAT_FINISH_MAX_TOOL_CALL_RETRIES` | `1` | Extra passes after a tool call the provider could not use (Gemini `MALFORMED_FUNCTION_CALL`), tools on offer. Only when no tool has run yet in the turn (the retry cannot see this turn's tool results, so it must not repeat a side effect); otherwise the person gets `malformed-tool-call-notice`. `0` never retries |
| `app.chat.finish.continue-nudge` | (none) | the shipped prompt `nudge.reply_cut_off` | **Deprecated alias** of the prompt `nudge.reply_cut_off` (sent as the next user message to make the model continue a reply cut off at the limit "exactly where it stopped"). Applied as the prompt's PLATFORM-scope default only when it differs from the shipped text, with a deprecation warning at startup; any stored override beats it. Not set in `application.yml` |
| `app.chat.finish.retry-tool-call-nudge` | (none) | the shipped prompt `nudge.retry_tool_call` | **Deprecated alias** of the prompt `nudge.retry_tool_call` (sent after a malformed tool call). Same rule as `continue-nudge`: the platform default only, warned at startup, beaten by any stored override. Not set in `application.yml` |
| `app.chat.finish.truncated-notice` | (none) | (none; the prompt `message.finish.truncated`) | Deprecated alias for the platform default of the prompt `message.finish.truncated`: shown after a reply that is still cut off when the continuation passes are used up. A value that differs from the shipped text is applied (and logged); a tenant or agent override of the prompt wins |
| `app.chat.finish.refusal-notice` | (none) | (none; the prompt `message.finish.refusal`) | Deprecated alias for the platform default of the prompt `message.finish.refusal`: shown when the provider's safety system stopped the reply (`{reason}` is the provider's own finish reason). Replaces the old "could you try rephrasing" line, which blamed the wording of the request for a block it did not cause |
| `app.chat.finish.malformed-tool-call-notice` | (none) | (none; the prompt `message.finish.malformed_tool_call`) | Deprecated alias for the platform default of the prompt `message.finish.malformed_tool_call`: shown when the model's tool call was unusable and the retries did not fix it: nothing was run |
| `app.chat.finish.empty-reply-message` | (none) | (none; the prompt `message.finish.empty_reply`) | Deprecated alias for the platform default of the prompt `message.finish.empty_reply`: shown when the model returned nothing at all and gave no reason (was the hard-coded "I don't have a response for that - could you try rephrasing your message?"). The reply is stored as a failed reply, not as something the agent said, and is never cached |
| `app.chat.resilience.tool-idle-ceiling` | `APP_CHAT_RESILIENCE_TOOL_IDLE_CEILING` | `PT30M` | Group `app.chat.resilience`. How long ONE tool may run before the stream idle timer counts it as hung, measured from the tool's last start, finish or progress. While a tool is in flight this replaces `app.llm.http.stream-idle-timeout`. The tools have their own timeouts; this is the backstop. Only tools wrapped by the platform's tool logging report their activity: a tool added by a caller as a raw extra tool is still timed like silence |
| `app.chat.resilience.replay-after-journaled-effects` | `APP_CHAT_RESILIENCE_REPLAY_AFTER_JOURNALED_EFFECTS` | `true` | A turn that already ran tools is replayed on another provider (the BYOK fallback provider, the next rung of the hosted pool) only when every tool that reached its implementation is read-only or was recorded by the effect journal, so that an identical repeat is served from the journal instead of the provider. A tool still running, a write/send tool that ran without a journal record (journal off, or the call threw after the provider may have acted) and an unclassified tool with a mutating name all block the replay; the provider's failure is then reported as it is. The journal protects an IDENTICAL repeat only (same tool and arguments within the turn): a replayed model that sends with different arguments is not protected, which is why `false` exists, meaning never replay after a tool with side effects ran. Counted in `chat.failover.blocked` |
| `app.chat.resilience.retry-at-fast-when-no-tool-ran` | `APP_CHAT_RESILIENCE_RETRY_AT_FAST_WHEN_NO_TOOL_RAN` | `true` | A careful / rigorous / planned turn that hits its time limit BEFORE any tool ran is answered again at fast depth with its tools on offer (nothing is lost; counted in `chat.degraded.depth_timeout`). `false` reports the timeout instead. A turn that hit the limit AFTER a tool ran is always reported with the steps that ran (`chat.degraded.depth_timeout_partial`), kept in the transcript so the next turn sees them, and is never cached |
| `app.chat.resilience.list-none` | (none) | `none` | Stands in for an empty list in the timeout notice |
| `app.chat.resilience.depth-timeout-partial-notice` | (none) | text in `application.yml` | Shown when a buffered-depth turn ran out of time after tools ran. `{depth}`, `{seconds}`, `{completed}` (tools that ran, with repeat counts) and `{running}` (tools still running) are filled in. Not shown to the model |
| `app.chat.resilience.depth-timeout-degraded-notice` | (none) | text in `application.yml` | Step-event note (`guardrail` event, subject `depth`) when a timed-out turn is answered again at fast depth; `{depth}` |
| `app.chat.resilience.replay-blocked-notice` | (none) | text in `application.yml` | Step-event note (`guardrail` event, subject `failover`) when a provider failure was not retried elsewhere because tools had run; `{tools}` |
| `app.agent-turn.repair.max-attempts` | `APP_AGENT_TURN_REPAIR_MAX_ATTEMPTS` | `1` | Group `app.agent-turn.repair`. `POST /api/completions/agent-turn` (hosted model for a client-side tool loop): extra model calls one turn may spend when a tool call arrives with unusable arguments (invalid or cut-off JSON). The model is handed the error as the call's result and asked again; usage of every call is summed at settlement and the reservation covers one call, so a repaired turn can cost up to `1 + max-attempts` calls. `0` never asks again. If the calls are still unusable they are dropped (never returned with empty arguments) and the reply text says so, with `finishReason` `length` or `malformed_tool_call` |
| `app.agent-turn.repair.invalid-arguments-message` | (none) | text in `application.yml` | Model-facing. The result handed to the model for the call whose arguments were unusable (`{tool}`, `{reason}`) |
| `app.agent-turn.repair.skipped-message` | (none) | text in `application.yml` | Model-facing. The result handed to the model for every other call of the same reply (none of them was run) (`{tool}`) |
| `app.agent-turn.repair.invalid-reason-cut-off` | (none) | text in `application.yml` | The `{reason}` when the arguments were cut off because the reply hit the output length limit |
| `app.agent-turn.repair.invalid-reason-malformed` | (none) | text in `application.yml` | The `{reason}` when the arguments were not valid JSON for another reason |
| `app.agent-turn.repair.unusable-calls-notice` | (none) | text in `application.yml` | Client-facing text returned when the calls are still unusable after the last attempt (`{tool}`, `{reason}`) |

The file tools `workspace_list`, `workspace_read`, `workspace_search`, `workspace_write` and `workspace_edit` are offered under the same
conditions as the terminal (enabled, one-person install) when the workspace folder exists in core's process. The
personal profile raises `max-timeout-seconds` to `1800`.

Compose (`--profile workspace`): `VEGADUTA_PROJECTS_DIR` (default `~/VegaDuta-Projects`), `WORKSPACE_RUNNER_SECRET`,
`WORKSPACE_RUNNER_MAX_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (`1800`), `WORKSPACE_RUNNER_MEM_LIMIT` (`3g`),
`WORKSPACE_RUNNER_PIDS_LIMIT` (`2048`: the most processes and threads the container may have at once, so a runaway command is stopped by the
container and not by the host; a Maven, Gradle or Next.js build peaks below 200; raise it only for a very large parallel build).

Network from the terminal follows the sandbox's per-server list: add `local-shell` (or `*`) to
`app.mcp.stdio.sandbox-network-allow`.

#### Limits that were constants (package 3c-2)

These were literals in the Java code; each is a setting now with the same default, so nothing changes until an operator sets one. The defaults are also in a `.properties` file in the owning module (the fallback when `application.yml` is not loaded); the two are kept equal.

**Agent max-tokens ceiling.** `app.agents.max-tokens-ceiling` is the one definition of "the most output tokens an agent's reply may be given". It bounds `POST /api/agents` and `PUT /api/agents/{id}/generation-params` (a larger `maxTokens` is a 400 whose message names the configured limit), the Simple-mode tuning that infers a value from the system prompt, and what one hosted-pool turn reserves of the tenant's monthly token budget (a stored value above the ceiling is reserved as the ceiling, so lowering it only ever tightens). It used to be the literal `8192` in `AgentCreateRequest`, `AgentGenerationParamsUpdateRequest`, `GenerationParamsInferenceService` and `FreeTierUsageLimiterService`. The web screens (`GenerationTuningSection.tsx`, `blueprintFields.ts`) still slide up to 8192: no existing API the console loads carries the ceiling, so raising it needs a new endpoint to follow. Lowering it is safe at once, because the server rejects the larger value and says why. Other copies of 8192 are not covered by this setting: `core/brief` (`BriefValidator`, `EnvoyAgentPorts`) and `ResearchRunPlan.AGENT_MAX_TOKENS`.

| Key | Env var | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.agents.max-tokens-ceiling` | `APP_AGENTS_MAX_TOKENS_CEILING` | `8192` | most output tokens an agent may be given (create, update, Simple-mode tuning, hosted-pool reservation) |
| `app.agents.inferred-max-tokens-floor` | `APP_AGENTS_INFERRED_MAX_TOKENS_FLOOR` | `128` | least value the Simple-mode tuning proposes (was `MIN_MAX_TOKENS`) |
| `app.agents.collaboration.max-branches` | `APP_AGENTS_COLLABORATION_MAX_BRANCHES` | `8` | branches one `delegate_parallel` or `run_subtasks` call may start; the rest come back as "over the limit" entries, and the swarm verifier re-dispatches at most this many per repair round. Was `MAX_BRANCHES` in `DelegateToTeamToolCallback` and `RunSubtasksToolCallback`. This is the platform value of the setting `delegation.maxBranches`: a workspace or an agent can override it in the console (docs/settings.md) |
| `app.agents.collaboration.branch-timeout-seconds` | `APP_AGENTS_COLLABORATION_BRANCH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | `240` | wall-clock limit of one branch (`BRANCH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, duplicated in both tools). The platform value of the setting `delegation.branchTimeoutSeconds` (workspace or agent override, 10-3600) |
| `app.agents.collaboration.max-answer-chars` | `APP_AGENTS_COLLABORATION_MAX_ANSWER_CHARS` | `4000` | characters of one branch's answer handed back to the calling model; a longer one is cut and ends with "…" (`MAX_ANSWER_CHARS`, duplicated in both tools) |
| `app.agents.collaboration.max-role-chars` | `APP_AGENTS_COLLABORATION_MAX_ROLE_CHARS` | `80` | characters kept of a `run_subtasks` worker's role label (`MAX_ROLE_CHARS`) |
| `app.agent-turn.limits.max-messages` | `APP_AGENT_TURN_LIMITS_MAX_MESSAGES` | `400` | messages in one `POST /api/completions/agent-turn` conversation; more is a 400 (`AgentTurnController.MAX_MESSAGES`) |
| `app.agent-turn.limits.max-total-chars` | `APP_AGENT_TURN_LIMITS_MAX_TOTAL_CHARS` | `600000` | characters of message content in that conversation; more is a 413 (`MAX_TOTAL_CHARS`) |
| `app.agent-turn.limits.max-tools` | `APP_AGENT_TURN_LIMITS_MAX_TOOLS` | `40` | tool definitions the client may offer; more is a 400 (`MAX_TOOLS`) |
| `app.agent-turn.limits.max-tool-calls-per-message` | `APP_AGENT_TURN_LIMITS_MAX_TOOL_CALLS_PER_MESSAGE` | `32` | tool calls on one assistant message; more is a 400 (`MAX_TOOL_CALLS_PER_MESSAGE`) |
| `app.llm.request-snapshot.max-message-chars` | `APP_LLM_REQUEST_SNAPSHOT_MAX_MESSAGE_CHARS` | `40000` | LLM request inspector: characters of one message kept (display only; was `LlmRequestSnapshotter.MAX_MESSAGE_CHARS`) |
| `app.llm.request-snapshot.max-total-chars` | `APP_LLM_REQUEST_SNAPSHOT_MAX_TOTAL_CHARS` | `300000` | characters of all messages kept (`MAX_TOTAL_CHARS`) |
| `app.llm.request-snapshot.max-schema-chars` | `APP_LLM_REQUEST_SNAPSHOT_MAX_SCHEMA_CHARS` | `4000` | characters of one tool's input schema kept (`MAX_SCHEMA_CHARS`) |
| `app.llm.request-snapshot.max-tools` | `APP_LLM_REQUEST_SNAPSHOT_MAX_TOOLS` | `200` | tool definitions kept (`MAX_TOOLS`) |
| `app.llm.request-snapshot.max-call-chars` | `APP_LLM_REQUEST_SNAPSHOT_MAX_CALL_CHARS` | `600` | characters of one tool call's arguments or one tool result kept (the literal `600`) |
| `app.llm.request-snapshot.truncation-marker` | (none) | `\n… [truncated for display]` | appended to a message that was cut |
| `app.llm.request-snapshot-store.mode` | `APP_LLM_REQUEST_SNAPSHOT_STORE_MODE` | `redis` | where the request inspector keeps its redacted copies of recent model requests: `redis` also writes each copy to Redis (the one the rate limiter uses), so any replica can answer "what was sent" for a turn another replica ran and the copies survive a restart for the ttl; `memory` is this JVM only (the behaviour before 2026-10). With `redis` and no Redis connection the store logs it and keeps memory only. Copies hold user text (redacted and capped), so they expire after the ttl in both places and are never written to a database |
| `app.llm.request-snapshot-store.ttl` | `APP_LLM_REQUEST_SNAPSHOT_STORE_TTL` | `6h` | how long a copy is kept, in memory and in Redis (was `LlmRequestSnapshotStore.TTL`) |
| `app.llm.request-snapshot-store.max-sessions` | `APP_LLM_REQUEST_SNAPSHOT_STORE_MAX_SESSIONS` | `300` | sessions kept in memory; the least recently used leave first (was `MAX_SESSIONS`) |
| `app.llm.request-snapshot-store.max-per-session` | `APP_LLM_REQUEST_SNAPSHOT_STORE_MAX_PER_SESSION` | `80` | model calls kept per session, in memory and in Redis (was `MAX_PER_SESSION`) |
| `app.llm.request-snapshot-store.write-queue-size` | `APP_LLM_REQUEST_SNAPSHOT_STORE_WRITE_QUEUE_SIZE` | `256` | copies waiting to be written to Redis; when full the oldest waiting copy is skipped (it stays in memory on the replica that took it), so a slow Redis never slows a model call |
| `app.llm.request-snapshot-store.key-prefix` | `APP_LLM_REQUEST_SNAPSHOT_STORE_KEY_PREFIX` | `agentic:llm-request-snapshots:` | Redis key prefix; the key is the prefix, the tenant, `\|` and the session id |
| `app.tools.mcp.request-timeout-seconds` | `APP_TOOLS_MCP_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | `60` | seconds one request to a tenant's registered MCP server (tool call, `tools/list`, initialize, health check) may wait (`DynamicMcpConnectionManager.REQUEST_TIMEOUT`). Applies to connections opened after the change. Separate from `app.agent-runtime`'s per-call tool timeout, which stops a call from outside |
| `app.tools.mcp.test-timeout-seconds` | `APP_TOOLS_MCP_TEST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | `20` | the same for the "test connection" action (`McpConnectionTester.TEST_TIMEOUT`) |
| `app.tools.openapi.connect-timeout-seconds` | `APP_TOOLS_OPENAPI_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | `10` | seconds an OpenAPI tool call, token call or spec fetch may take to connect (`OpenApiToolExecutor.CONNECT_TIMEOUT`) |
| `app.tools.openapi.request-timeout-seconds` | `APP_TOOLS_OPENAPI_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | `30` | seconds such a call may take to get its reply (`REQUEST_TIMEOUT`) |
| `app.tools.openapi.max-response-bytes` | `APP_TOOLS_OPENAPI_MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES` | `1048576` | bytes of an API reply that are read (`MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES`) |
| `app.tools.openapi.max-result-chars` | `APP_TOOLS_OPENAPI_MAX_RESULT_CHARS` | `16000` | characters of an API reply handed to the model; a longer one is clipped and the result says so (`MAX_RESULT_CHARS`) |
| `app.tools.openapi.max-spec-redirects` | `APP_TOOLS_OPENAPI_MAX_SPEC_REDIRECTS` | `3` | redirects followed when fetching a spec; each hop is re-checked against the SSRF guard (`MAX_SPEC_REDIRECTS`) |

The groups above are `app.agents` (`max-tokens-ceiling`, `inferred-max-tokens-floor`), `app.agents.collaboration`, `app.agent-turn.limits`, `app.llm.request-snapshot`, `app.llm.request-snapshot-store`, `app.tools.mcp` and `app.tools.openapi`.

The tool-call ceiling per turn and its autonomy-tier scaling (supervised divisor and floor, autonomous multiplier) are `app.guardrails.max-tool-calls-per-turn` and `app.guardrails.step-limit.*`, and the knowledge-search top-k and minimum score are `app.agent-runtime.defaults.retrieval-top-k` / `retrieval-min-score` (and the agent and tenant overrides of them); both were made settings by earlier packages and are unchanged here.

### The Assistant's desktop tools

`local_desktop_*` — computer-use on the person's own screen through the **desktop-agent** sidecar
(`services/desktop-agent`), which runs ON the laptop (never a container): screenshots, the
accessibility element tree, click/type/key, open, notifications and clipboard. Offered only on a
one-person install while `agent-url` is set — the URL being configured IS the opt-in.

| Property | Env | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.assistant.desktop.agent-url` | `APP_ASSISTANT_DESKTOP_AGENT_URL` | empty | The sidecar (`http://127.0.0.1:8787`, or `http://host.docker.internal:8787` when core runs in Docker) |
| `app.assistant.desktop.agent-secret` | `APP_ASSISTANT_DESKTOP_AGENT_SECRET` | empty | Bearer secret; must match the sidecar's `DESKTOP_AGENT_SECRET` |
| `app.assistant.desktop.approval-required` | `APP_ASSISTANT_DESKTOP_APPROVAL_REQUIRED` | `true` (`false` in the `personal` profile) | Pin `local_desktop_press/click/type/key/open/activate` **and `run_desktop_recipe`** for approval — these actions are NOT sandboxed. `false` lets them run like ordinary tools; a `SUPERVISED` agent still asks |
| `app.assistant.desktop.recipes.enabled` | `DESKTOP_RECIPES_ENABLED` | `false` | `run_desktop_recipe` — record once, replay a saved click/type/open sequence on the same screen through the sidecar. Keep it OUT of `app.tools.classification.confirmation-gate-exempt-tools`: the model supplies `confirmIrreversible`/`fromStep` itself, so the human approval of the call is the real check |
| `app.assistant.desktop.recipes.max-per-tenant` | `DESKTOP_RECIPES_MAX_PER_TENANT` | `50` | saved recipes per tenant |
| `app.assistant.desktop.recipes.step-timeout-ms` | `DESKTOP_RECIPES_STEP_TIMEOUT_MS` | `15000` | one replayed step's budget |
| `app.assistant.desktop.recipes.max-replay-seconds` | `DESKTOP_RECIPES_MAX_REPLAY_SECONDS` | `120` | whole-replay wall-clock cap |
| `app.assistant.desktop.recipes.monitor-step-delay-ms` | `DESKTOP_RECIPES_MONITOR_STEP_DELAY_MS` | `800` | MONITOR mode's pause between steps, so a person can follow the replay on the live monitor |
| `app.assistant.desktop.recipes.secret-name-prefix` | `DESKTOP_RECIPES_SECRET_NAME_PREFIX` | `DESKTOP_` | `{{secret.NAME}}` in a recipe's typed text resolves at replay through the secret vault under this prefix (mirrors `app.browser.secret-name-prefix`) |
| `app.assistant.desktop.monitor.enabled` | `DESKTOP_MONITOR_ENABLED` | `false` | the live monitor (`GET /api/desktop/monitor/frame` proxies the sidecar's `GET /frame`) — every poll captures the whole screen, so it stays opt-in |

Scheduled tasks (`app.scheduled-tasks.enabled`, `SCHEDULED_TASKS_ENABLED`) are `true` in the
`personal` profile — the recurring "check X every morning and ping me" loop; delivery
`whatsapp_qr` sends through the linked WhatsApp QR channel. See
[Personal mode](personal-mode.md#scheduled-tasks).

`SCHEDULED_TASKS_DELIVERY_CHANNELS` picks which destinations the form and API accept
(default enables all): `in_app`, `email`, `whatsapp`, `whatsapp_qr`, `telegram`, `slack`,
`signal`, `matrix`, `imessage`, `a2a`, `voice`. The channel sends go through the normal
approval policy; `a2a` delivers to a remote agent's card URL through the outbound A2A client
(needs `APP_A2A_CLIENT_ENABLED=true` and the host in `APP_A2A_CLIENT_ALLOWED_HOSTS`), and
`voice` places an outbound call that speaks the result (needs the tenant's Twilio voice
credential, trimmed to ~900 spoken characters).

### Skills

| Property | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `app.skills.learning.enabled` | `false` | the learning loop: after a completed multi-step turn that used no skill, draft one and park it as pending for a person to approve. `true` in `personal` |
| `app.learning.proposals.enabled` | `false` | the learning-proposal store behind every "the assistant learns" loop: lessons, preferences and the like wait here as PENDING until the person approves them (`GET /api/learning/proposals`, `POST .../{id}/approve`, `.../reject`); nothing reaches a prompt, a schedule or a stored preference before approval. `true` in `personal`; off, the routes 404 |
| `app.learning.lessons.enabled` | `false` | the lessons loop: a tool call that failed and was retried with different arguments, or a user message that corrects the previous answer, becomes one short imperative lesson (one small model call on the agent's own model, off the request thread, scrubbed, per-agent cooldown and daily cap from `app.learning.proposals.*`) filed as a PENDING lesson proposal. Needs `app.learning.proposals.enabled`. `true` in `personal` |
| `app.learning.proposals.max-pending` | `50` | pending proposals one person can have; further ones are dropped until some are decided |
| `app.learning.proposals.ttl-days` | `30` | pending proposals older than this expire |
| `app.learning.proposals.cooldown-minutes` | `30` | minimum time between two learning runs for the same agent and loop |
| `app.learning.proposals.max-per-agent-per-day` | `6` | learning runs per agent and loop in any 24 hours |
| `app.learning.preferences.enabled` | `false` | the preferences-from-behavior loop (needs `app.learning.proposals.enabled`): counts repeated style requests in chat messages and thumbs comments ("shorter", "no emojis", "reply in Hindi") and the person's own approve/reject habits, and files a PREFERENCE proposal once a signal repeats. No model call. Approval habits are informational only and never change an approval rule. `true` in `personal` |
| `app.learning.preferences.threshold` | `3` | how many times a signal must repeat within the window before it is proposed (minimum 2) |
| `app.learning.preferences.window-days` | `30` | the counting window, in days (1 to 365) |
| `app.learning.routines.enabled` | `false` | routines from habits: requests you repeat on a rhythm (same intent on at least 3 distinct days, at about the same time; daily, every weekday or weekly) become a ROUTINE proposal that, once approved, creates a scheduled task. Needs `app.learning.proposals.enabled` and `app.scheduled-tasks.enabled`. Your requests are kept scrubbed (one that needed scrubbing is never kept) for `retention-days`, in `routine_observations` (V633); no model call. `true` in `personal` |
| `app.learning.routines.timezone` | server zone | the time zone "around 9am" is read in and the proposed task runs in (e.g. `Asia/Kolkata`); env `APP_LEARNING_ROUTINES_TIMEZONE` |
| `app.learning.routines.similarity` | `0.6` | how alike two requests must be (shared content words, 0.3-0.95) to count as the same intent |
| `app.learning.routines.min-occurrences` / `min-distinct-days` | `3` / `3` | evidence needed before proposing (neither goes below 3) |
| `app.learning.routines.max-time-spread-minutes` | `120` | the first request of each day must fall within this window to count as "the same time" |
| `app.learning.routines.window-days` / `retention-days` | `28` / `45` | how far back requests are compared / how long they are kept |
| `app.learning.review.enabled` | `false` | the daily memory review (needs `app.learning.proposals.enabled`): once per person per day, off the chat path and with no model call, it compares your remembered facts and files CONSOLIDATION proposals for near-duplicates (keep the more informative), contradictions (keep the newer) and facts unused for `stale-days` (forget). Approving performs the merge or forget through the Memory services; nothing changes before. Also serves `GET /api/learning/review/summary` (what was learned this week). `true` in `personal` |
| `app.learning.review.stale-days` | `60` | a fact not saved or recalled for this many days is offered for forgetting (minimum 7) |
| `app.learning.review.max-per-run` | `5` | most proposals one review of one person files |
| `app.learning.review.max-people-per-tick` | `200` | most people one hourly tick reviews in a tenant |
| `app.skills.learning.self-check.enabled` | `false` | before a drafted skill is queued, one extra model call (the agent's own model, off the request thread, inside the same per-agent cooldown and daily cap) judges whether following it would have answered the originating request at least as well. The verdict (PASS, WEAK) is stored with the pending skill (V653) and shown as a "checked" badge in the Learned section and the skill library; a FAIL draft is dropped and logged; no verdict (call failed, limit hit) queues it unchecked. `true` in `personal` |
| `app.skills.learning.min-tool-calls` | `3` | tool calls a turn needs before it is considered (minimum 2) |
| `app.skills.learning.cooldown-minutes` | `30` | per agent, between drafting attempts |
| `app.skills.learning.max-drafts-per-agent-per-day` | `3` | per agent drafting attempts (model calls, not stored drafts) |
| `app.skills.learning.max-transcript-chars` | `6000` | transcript sent to the model (1,000 to 20,000) |
| `app.skills.learning.stale-days` | `90` | a skill unused this long is flagged stale; used by the lifecycle view even with the loop off |

At most 10 drafts wait for review per workspace. See [Personal mode](personal-mode.md#skills).

### Edition tier and features

core runs as one edition, `app.edition.tier` (`APP_EDITION_TIER`): `personal`, `lite` or `full`. The `personal` profile sets `personal`; with no tier set, or `lite` or `full`, every feature below is on, so Lite, Full and the worker and jobs shapes are unchanged. In Compose you do not set the tier itself: `VEGADUTA_EDITION` in `deploy/compose/.env` feeds both core's `APP_EDITION_TIER` and the console's `NEXT_PUBLIC_EDITION` (`deploy/compose/personal.yml`).

A feature that is off is not registered at all: its controllers, services, schedulers and agent tools do not exist on that core, and its endpoints answer 404. Tables, entities, repositories and migrations are not affected, so turning a feature on later finds its data intact. Each feature is decided once, at boot.

| Feature id | Personal | What it covers |
|---|---|---|
| `billing-payments` | off | payment gateways (Razorpay, Stripe, PhonePe), checkout, invoices, subscriptions and upgrades, budget top-ups, the agent wallet, payment mandates, the commerce feed (`/api/commerce/feed`) and the `create_payment_link`, `request_upi_collect` and `check_payment_status` agent tools. The x402 seller endpoints answer "payments disabled" |
| `social-channels` | off | the Slack, Discord, Microsoft Teams and X channel adapters, webhooks and credential endpoints |
| `a2a-directory` | off | the A2A JSON-RPC and REST endpoints, agent cards and the public agent directory |
| `mcp-server-exposure` | off | `/api/dev/v1` and `/api/mcpserver/exposure`. The grants endpoint stays registered while `app.openai-compat.enabled=true` |
| `operator-console` | off | the platform operator endpoints for tenants, users, plans, spend, abuse, moderation, fleet, impersonation, audit and ops, plus the demo, newsletter and landing surfaces. The platform LLM pool, tool credentials and platform settings are not part of it |
| `cloud-speech` | off | the Sarvam and ElevenLabs integrations (speech, voice cloning, music). An add-on: switch it on, restart core, add the key in the console |
| `voice-realtime-share` | off | anonymous realtime voice on share links, call recording and the LiveKit egress |
| `video-demo-media` | off | Vertex Veo video generation and the demo image and video endpoints |

How each feature is decided, first match wins:

1. `app.edition.features.<feature id>.enabled` (environment: `APP_EDITION_FEATURES_<ID>_ENABLED`, the id with `-` written as `_`, for example `APP_EDITION_FEATURES_CLOUD_SPEECH_ENABLED=true`). It is read as a boolean, so `true`/`false`, `1`/`0`, `on`/`off` and `yes`/`no` work; a blank value counts as unset. Put it in `deploy/compose/core.env` and restart core.
2. `billing-payments` only: `app.edition.billing-enabled` (`APP_EDITION_BILLING_ENABLED`, default `true`, see below).
3. Otherwise the tier: `personal` is off, anything else (or no tier) is on.

Rules of the road: a gated bean is only injected by beans gated by the same feature, or through `ObjectProvider`, `Optional` or a collection (`EditionClosureRulesTest` checks it from bytecode); a new gated bean needs nothing else, because `EditionWiringTest` finds them by annotation.

`GET /api/public/status` reports the result in an `edition` object: `{tier, billingEnabled, authMode, features}`, where `features` maps each id above to `true` or `false`. It is computed by the same code as the bean conditions. The console takes its tier from `NEXT_PUBLIC_EDITION` and, once the status arrives, warns and raises the platform status banner if it differs from core's.

### Billing edition

| Property | Env | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.edition.billing-enabled` | `APP_EDITION_BILLING_ENABLED` | `true` | `false`: no plans, prices, payments, billing mail or upgrade prompts; the billing, checkout, invoice and top-up endpoints are not registered; every limit resolves as unlimited. Usage is still counted. This is the `billing-payments` [edition feature](#edition-tier-and-features): an explicit `app.edition.features.billing-payments.enabled` wins over it. The console follows `NEXT_PUBLIC_BILLING_ENABLED` separately |

### Local models

| Property | Env | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.local-models.ollama-base-url` | (none) | `app.local-llm.bootstrap.base-url`, then `http://ollama:11434` | the only Ollama the local-model API talks to; no request parameter can change it. A loopback or Compose-DNS address must also be in `APP_SECURITY_OUTBOUND_ALLOWED_PRIVATE_HOSTS` |
| `app.local-models.host-ram-gb` | `HOST_RAM_GB` | `0` | the host's memory in GB, set by `scripts/vegaduta personal` (and `scripts\vegaduta.ps1 personal` on Windows, from the computer, not the WSL2 VM) for a containerised core (which would otherwise report its own cgroup limit). `0` measures what core sees |
| `app.local-models.host-accelerator` | `HOST_ACCELERATOR` | blank | `apple`, or `nvidia:NAME:VRAM_GB` (from `nvidia-smi`, also on Windows): what the host has, for the same reason. Blank detects from inside core |
| `app.local-models.allow-uncataloged-pull` | (none) | `false` | allow pulling tags that are not in the catalog |
| `app.local-models.keep-alive` | `APP_LOCAL_MODELS_KEEP_ALIVE` | `30m`; the Compose `local-ai` overlay sets it and Ollama's own `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE` from `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE` (default `-1`) | Ollama `keep_alive` sent by `POST /api/local-models/warm` (and by the warm-ups core runs itself after a pull, a `/use` switch and the local-LLM bootstrap): how long the loaded model stays in memory. Ollama's own default is five minutes, after which the next chat pays the whole weight load again; `-1` keeps it forever. A chat request sent without the field resets the timer to the server's default, so for a host Ollama also set `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE` on the server (the Compose `local-ai` overlay already does) |
| `app.llm.ollama.reasoning-effort` | `APP_LLM_OLLAMA_REASONING_EFFORT` | `none` for the install's own local model (the platform-pool row Providers -> Local model manages, which can override it per row); blank for agents on an `OLLAMA` provider. The Compose `local-ai` overlay sets `none` (`LOCAL_LLM_REASONING_EFFORT`) | `reasoning_effort` sent to Ollama's OpenAI-compat endpoint: `none` skips the hidden thinking pass of reasoning models (qwen3.5, qwen3, deepseek-r1, gpt-oss), which on a laptop is most of the wait (a one-line question took 40+ s on `qwen3.5:4b` against about 5 s with it off); `low`/`medium`/`high` leave it on at that effort; blank sends nothing (the model's default). An unsupported name falls back to the model's default, so it cannot fail a call |
| `app.local-models.rate-limit.reads-per-minute` | (none) | `120` | per tenant and caller |
| `app.local-models.rate-limit.probes-per-minute` | (none) | `6` | |
| `app.local-models.rate-limit.pulls-per-minute` | (none) | `6` | |
| `app.llm.ollama.num-ctx` | `APP_LLM_OLLAMA_NUM_CTX` | `32768` | `num_ctx` sent to Ollama per request (its OpenAI-compat `options` field) - the provider default (~4k) silently truncates tool-heavy prompts |
| `app.llm.ollama.num-batch` | `APP_LLM_OLLAMA_NUM_BATCH` | `0` (server default 512) | `num_batch` sent to Ollama per request - tokens evaluated per prompt step; a larger value (e.g. `2048`, keep `<= num-ctx`) speeds up the multi-thousand-token fixed-prefix eval that dominates turn latency on a local model |
| `app.llm.structured-output.ollama-format.enabled` | (none) | `false` | when on, a structured-output request that Ollama refuses (the MLX runner's `json_schema` 400) is retried with the schema in the prompt |

A `LOCAL_OPENAI` provider (vLLM, llama.cpp, LM Studio, Jan, LocalAI) adds no keys. Its server must be listed in
`app.security.outbound.allowed-private-hosts` (for example `localhost:8000`, or `host.docker.internal:1234` when core
runs in Docker), and `app.llm.tool-call-probe.enabled` (default `true`) governs its tool-calling check.

### Channels and inbound limits

| Property | Env | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.signal.receive.enabled` | (none) | `true` | the receiver is idle until a number is connected; never runs in the jobs or worker shapes |
| `app.signal.receive.mode` | (none) | `auto` | `poll` or `websocket` force one |
| `app.signal.receive.poll-seconds` | (none) | `15` | long-poll length, 1 to 25 |
| `app.signal.max-message-chars` / `app.signal.max-attachment-bytes` | (none) | `3500` / `20971520` | |
| `app.whatsappqr.bridge-url` / `app.whatsappqr.bridge-secret` | `APP_WHATSAPPQR_BRIDGE_URL` / `APP_WHATSAPPQR_BRIDGE_SECRET` | empty / empty | the whatsapp-qr bridge address and shared token; both empty hides the channel - see [WhatsApp QR](channels/whatsapp-qr.md) |
| `app.whatsappqr.receive.enabled` | `APP_WHATSAPPQR_RECEIVE_ENABLED` | `true` | the receiver is idle until a session is linked; one process per bridge |
| `app.whatsappqr.receive.poll-seconds` | (none) | `15` | long-poll length, 1 to 20 |
| `app.whatsappqr.max-message-chars` | (none) | `60000` | |
| `app.matrix.sync.enabled` | `APP_MATRIX_SYNC_ENABLED` | `false` | run the `/sync` loop in this process; one process per bot. The `personal` profile does not set it |
| `app.imessage.polling.enabled` | (none) | `false` | start the BlueBubbles polling fallback |
| `app.imessage.polling.interval-ms` | (none) | `5000` | poll period (minimum 1000) |
| `app.imessage.rate-limit.inbound-per-minute` | (none) | `20` | per chat; `0` disables |
| `app.imessage.rate-limit.outbound-per-minute` | (none) | `30` | per tenant; `0` disables |
| `app.omnichannel.rate-limit.enabled` | `APP_OMNICHANNEL_RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED` | `false` (`true` in `personal`) | opt-in cap on inbound turns for WhatsApp, Telegram, Slack, Discord, Teams, Meta social, X, email and SMS (voice is exempt) |
| `app.omnichannel.rate-limit.per-sender-per-minute` | (none) | `20` | per tenant, channel and sender; `0` disables the counter. On Slack, Discord, Teams and X the "sender" is the channel or conversation |
| `app.omnichannel.rate-limit.per-channel-per-minute` | (none) | `600` | per tenant and channel; `0` disables |

An over-limit message is dropped without a reply on the channel, costs no session write or model call, and the API
answers `429` with `Retry-After`. Signal, Matrix, iMessage, the WhatsApp QR bridge and an email (IMAP) mailbox reach private hosts only through
`app.security.outbound.allowed-private-hosts` (for example `signal-cli:8080`, `whatsapp-qr:8080`, the Mac's `host:port`, a LAN
homeserver, a mail server on your own network). The IMAP host and port a tenant enters are checked before any connection
is opened and again on every poll; a stored mailbox that is no longer allowed is skipped with a WARN naming it.

### Voice sockets and guardrails

| Property | Env | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.voice.realtime.allowed-origins` | (none) | `${app.web.allowed-origins}` plus same-origin | origins that may open the browser realtime-voice socket; `*` allows any |
| `app.voice.relay.allowed-origins` | (none) | empty | origins on the Twilio ConversationRelay socket; empty is a same-origin check only, which admits Twilio (it sends no `Origin`) and refuses browser pages |
| `app.guardrails.fail-mode` | `APP_GUARDRAILS_FAIL_MODE` | `closed` | `closed`: a guardrail check that itself fails refuses the call or withholds the output. `open` restores the old fail-open behaviour and logs a warning. **Behaviour change:** a per-agent tool-argument policy lookup that throws (`app.tool-arg-policies`) used to let the call through in every mode; it now follows this setting, so with the default a failing policy store refuses the agent's tool calls with a "could not be checked" result instead of silently dropping the policies |
| `app.guardrails.judge.max-input-chars` | `APP_GUARDRAILS_JUDGE_MAX_INPUT_CHARS` | `4000` | Group `app.guardrails.judge`. Characters of the checked text (a tool result, a user message, tool-call arguments) put in front of an LLM judge: the tool-output injection judge, the toxicity judge, the RIGOROUS tool-call verifier and the moderation policy-pack classifier. The rest is not judged. Was a constant in each of the four classes |
| `app.guardrails.judge.truncation-marker` | (none) | ` …[truncated]` | Appended to judge input cut at `max-input-chars` (model-facing; the leading space is part of the value) |
| `app.guardrails.judge.temperature` | `APP_GUARDRAILS_JUDGE_TEMPERATURE` | empty (inherit) | Sampling temperature of judge calls. Empty keeps the calling agent's own setting, which is what the judges used before this existed (an agent at 0.9 gets a noisy verdict); `0.0` pins judges. Sent as per-call options |
| `app.guardrails.judge.max-tokens` | `APP_GUARDRAILS_JUDGE_MAX_TOKENS` | empty (inherit) | Output-token cap of judge calls. Empty keeps the agent's own cap (an agent with a very small cap can truncate a verdict); the verdicts are one line |
| `app.guardrails.judge.model` | `APP_GUARDRAILS_JUDGE_MODEL` | empty (agent's model) | Model id for judge calls, a per-call override on the same provider and credentials as the agent's chat client. It does not switch provider. Not yet applied to the answer-consistency and Reflexion judges, which live in `core/agents-turn` |
| `app.guardrails.jailbreak.max-scanned-chars` | `APP_GUARDRAILS_JAILBREAK_MAX_SCANNED_CHARS` | `16000` | Characters of a message or reply the jailbreak patterns scan. Text beyond it is ignored, not rejected, so a payload padded past the window is not seen; raise it to close that (the patterns are bounded, the cost is linear). The chat gateway's prompt-injection screener has its own window |
| `app.guardrails.toxicity.block-score` | `APP_GUARDRAILS_TOXICITY_BLOCK_SCORE` | `60` | The toxicity judge's 0-100 score at or above which content is blocked. Lower blocks more |
| `app.guardrails.tool-verifier.fail-mode` | `APP_GUARDRAILS_TOOL_VERIFIER_FAIL_MODE` | `inherit` | What the RIGOROUS tool-call verifier does when its model call errors or answers nothing. `inherit`: follow `app.guardrails.fail-mode` (`closed` unless changed), so the verifier agrees with the injection judge and the dispatch gate; `open`: approve the call; `closed`: refuse it. **Behaviour change:** the verifier used to fail open always (including on a blank answer); with the default it now refuses the call. A missing chat client is "not configured" and approves in every mode |
| `app.guardrails.tool-verifier.unavailable-reason` | (none) | `the tool-call check could not be completed - failed closed` | Model-facing. The reason the verifier gives for refusing a call it could not check (fail-closed only) |
| `app.guardrails.tool-verifier.context-messages` | `APP_GUARDRAILS_TOOL_VERIFIER_CONTEXT_MESSAGES` | `6` | How many of the latest messages of the conversation (user and assistant, oldest first, within `app.guardrails.judge.max-input-chars`) the RIGOROUS verifier is shown in front of the current user message, read from the turn's session memory under its scoped id. A follow-up such as "yes, send it" used to be judged against "yes". `0` = the current message alone (the old behaviour). The framing line is the prompt key `judge.tool_verifier.context` |
| `app.guardrails.supervised.gate-read-only` | `APP_GUARDRAILS_SUPERVISED_GATE_READ_ONLY` | `true` (`false` in the `personal` profile) | Group `app.guardrails.supervised`. `true`: the `SUPERVISED` autonomy tier asks a human before every tool call, a search or a lookup included (the behaviour before this existed). `false`: a call runs without approval only when ALL hold: the tool has an explicit classification row that says read-only (a stored row or a compiled-in declaration; never an unclassified tool, a quarantined one, or an MCP server's own read-only hint), the agent is positively judged not to hold the lethal trifecta (`app.tools.classification.trifecta-*`; an agent that does, or that cannot be judged, keeps full gating), and nothing else asks for approval (taint, a procedure, a hook, the confirmation gate). Argument policies, hooks, budget and step limits, the circuit breaker and every other guard still run on the call. Each such call is written to the audit log (`tool.supervised.auto_allowed`) and to the turn journal as `auto-allowed: read-only under SUPERVISED`, and counted in `agent.tool.supervised.auto_allowed`. A security setting (`guardrails.supervised.readOnlyCalls` in the [settings catalog](settings.md)): a workspace administrator or an agent can only choose `gate`, never loosen a platform `true`. An Auto-select agent (empty tool allow-list) is judged by the trifecta analysis only when `app.tools.classification.trifecta-analyse-auto-select` is `true` |
| `app.guardrails.approval-park.awaiting-message` | (none) | text in `application.yml` | Group `app.guardrails.approval-park` (model-facing instructions, editable). What the model reads when its tool call is held for human approval: the action is awaiting approval and was not run, the task resumes automatically once it is decided, tell the user briefly and end the turn without abandoning the goal. `{tool}` and `{requestId}` are filled in Compatibility alias of the prompt `toolresult.park.awaiting` ([prompts](prompts.md)): an override of the key at any scope wins, then this value when it differs from the shipped wording, then the shipped wording. |
| `app.guardrails.approval-park.queued-message` | (none) | text in `application.yml` | What the model reads for a tool call it makes after the turn parked: the call was not run, was recorded as its own pending approval and runs once approved. `{tool}`, `{requestId}`, `{parkedTool}` Alias of `toolresult.park.queued`, same order. |
| `app.guardrails.approval-park.queue-full-message` | (none) | text in `application.yml` | The same, when `max-queued-calls` calls are already queued: the call was neither run nor queued Alias of `toolresult.park.queue_full`, same order. |
| `app.guardrails.approval-park.record-failed-message` | (none) | text in `application.yml` | What the model reads when the approval request itself could not be recorded (database error): nothing is waiting, the call was not run, the turn is not parked. `{tool}` Alias of `toolresult.park.record_failed`, same order. |
| `app.guardrails.approval-park.max-queued-calls` | `APP_GUARDRAILS_APPROVAL_PARK_MAX_QUEUED_CALLS` | `5` | Calls made after a turn parked on an approval that are recorded as their own pending approvals (so they run once approved instead of being lost). Further calls are refused unrecorded; `0` queues none |
| `app.guardrails.approval-park.max-goal-chars` | `APP_GUARDRAILS_APPROVAL_PARK_MAX_GOAL_CHARS` | `4000` | How much of the user message that started a turn parked on an approval is stored in the approval's replay envelope, so the task can be continued with its goal restated after the decision. Longer messages are cut with a marker. `0` stores no goal: the request stays replayable but the platform does not continue the task |
| `app.guardrails.approval-park.goal-cut-marker` | (none) | ` [...message shortened]` | Appended to a recorded goal cut at `max-goal-chars` (model-facing; the leading space is part of the value), so the agent knows the message it is shown was shortened |
| `app.approvals.expiry.ttl` | `APP_APPROVALS_EXPIRY_TTL` | `0` (off) | How long an approval request may stay pending, from its creation. `0` (default): never expires, as before. When set, a sweep closes older pending requests: stored as `REJECTED` by `system:approval-expiry` with `expired-reason` as the reason (no new status value, so the API, SDK and UI contracts are unchanged), audited as `EXPIRED`, announced as `approval.resolved` with decision `expired`. A later approve is refused as already decided, so a stale action can no longer be approved into running; an executor with rejection cleanup (a halted workflow node) is told; a parked agent job fails as `APPROVAL_TIMEOUT` (or, with `jobs-continue-after-denial`, resumes with the expiry as the result). Keep it at least as long as `app.agent-jobs.approval.park-timeout` if the job should time out first |
| `app.approvals.expiry.sweep-interval` | `APP_APPROVALS_EXPIRY_SWEEP_INTERVAL` | `PT5M` | How often the expiry sweep runs (one replica at a time, every tenant schema). Does nothing while `ttl` is `0` |
| `app.approvals.expiry.initial-delay` | `APP_APPROVALS_EXPIRY_INITIAL_DELAY` | `PT1M` | Delay after startup before the first sweep |
| `app.approvals.expiry.batch-size` | `APP_APPROVALS_EXPIRY_BATCH_SIZE` | `200` | Most requests expired per tenant per sweep; the rest wait for the next tick |
| `app.approvals.expiry.expired-reason` | (none) | text in `application.yml` | Model-facing. The rejection reason recorded on an expired request and read back by the agent that asked: the action was not performed, the request can no longer be approved, ask again or take another route. `{ttl}` is the wait, for example `24 hours` |
| `app.approvals.continuation.enabled` | `APP_APPROVALS_CONTINUATION_ENABLED` | `true` | After a human approves, fails or denies a gated tool call the platform continues the original task by itself: for a chat session it starts a turn (same agent, session and person) that restates the user's goal and hands the agent the tool's result; an agent job's resumed turn is built from the same message. `false`: a decision only runs or refuses the tool and writes the history note |
| `app.approvals.continuation.max-result-chars` | `APP_APPROVALS_CONTINUATION_MAX_RESULT_CHARS` | `16000` | Longest tool output (or failure text, or denial reason) put into the continuation message; longer output is cut and says how much was left out. The approval request itself keeps up to 32 KiB. The continuation message is stored in the conversation like any other turn, so what it carries is replayed to the model on later turns while it stays inside the history window: keep this as small as the tools you gate allow. `0` sends no output |
